### Production lines

1. **[Bengaluru](44275267.md) Plant I:** HAL's first Tejas production line is situated at [Bangalore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangalore) near [HAL Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HAL_Airport). The production line was set up at a cost of [₹](490783.md)1,556 [crore](121420.md) (equivalent to ₹27 billion or US\$280 million in 2026), with HAL holding 50% stake and the rest held by IAF and Navy. First serial production aircraft delivered in 2015 with production rate of 8 per year.

   [^cite_note-\:57-212]

   [^cite_note-213]

2. **Bengaluru Plant II:** In March 2017, Government of India sanctioned ₹1,381.04 crore (equivalent to ₹21 billion or US\$220 million in 2026) for setting up an additional production line under Aircraft Division, HAL. This would increase the production capacity from 8 to 16. Production of sub-components like Front Fuselage, Centre Fuselage, Rear Fuselage, Wings were outsourced to private partners.

   [^cite_note-Orders-179]

   &#x20;The production plant was inaugurated by the [Defence Minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minister_of_Defence_\(India\)), [Rajnath Singh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rajnath_Singh), on 2 February 2021.

   [^cite_note-\:61-192]

3. **[Nashik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nashik_Airport) Plant:** On 5 April 2023, the then [Defence Secretary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defence_Secretary_\(India\)), [Giridhar Aramane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giridhar_Aramane), laid the foundation stone for a third production line at HAL, Nashik with an investment of over ₹150 crore (equivalent to ₹164 crore or US\$17 million in 2026). This would bring the total production capacity from 16 to 24 aircraft annually. The production of aircraft at this plant would start from October 2024

   [^cite_note-Aramane-214]

   [^cite_note-215]

   &#x20;with the first example then expected by the end of April 2025.

   [^cite_note-\:60-216]

   &#x20;The first Tejas Mk 1A took its first flight on 17 October 2024 after being inaugurated by the Defence Minister, Rajnath Singh.

   [^cite_note-\:74-217]

   [^cite_note-\:75-218]

   1. The facility is spread on 1,300,000 sq ft (120,000 m

      2

      ) and employs 30 structural assembly jigs for all major aircraft modules like the centre fuselage, front fuselage, rear fuselage, wings and air intake. The facility outsources 40% of its workshare to private firms and plans to enhance annual aircraft output to 10 in two years.

      [^cite_note-\:73-219]

      [^cite_note-220]

4. **Nashik Plant II:** HAL also plans to setup another production line at Nashik to ramp up production capacity from 24 to 32 as reported on 10 January 2025. The line can start production within one and a half years of sanction.

   [^cite_note-\:56-182]

   &#x20;The establishment of the plant is subject to a regular supply of GE engines.

   [^cite_note-\:62-221]

### Delivery

#### Mark 1 contracts

On 1 October 2014, LCA-SP1 aircraft took the first flight. On 17 January 2015, the aircraft was delivered to the Indian Air Force. Left head till then flow on 5 sorties to test various equipment. Due to delays in production the cost per aircraft rose from [₹](490783.md)120 [crore](121420.md) (equivalent to ₹421 crore or US\$44 million in 2026) in 2006 to ₹160 crore (equivalent to ₹263 crore or US\$27 million in 2026) in 2015.[^cite_note-222] However, the aircraft was not inducted into a squadron till 2016 until sufficient numbers were delivered by HAL. The aircraft would also require additional flight hours before induction. Expected delivery timeline was as follows: SP-2 in March, up to SP-5 by June, up to SP-8 by March 2016; completing a total of 8 aircraft.[^cite_note-\:57-212]

The formation of the first Tejas-equipped squadron started in July 2011. [No. 45 Squadron IAF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/No._45_Squadron_IAF) *(Flying Daggers)*, the first Tejas squadron, was operationalised in July 2016. It is based in [Sulur Air Force Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sulur_Air_Force_Station), [Coimbatore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coimbatore).[^cite_note-HinduTejasSqn2-73][^cite_note-223]

When the first order was placed in 2006, it was expected that ADA would receive Initial Operational Clearance (IOC) by 2008. Induction was scheduled by December 2011. However, IOC was originally received in December 2013 and the first IOC variant was delivered in FY2016-17.[^cite_note-Orders-179]

As of 11 February 2019, 12 IOC aircraft were delivered with 4 due to be delivered by March 2019. Also, on 31 December 2018, ADA has handed over Drawing Applicability List (DAL) and Equipment Standard of Preparation (SOP) documents, for FOC configuration to HAL for production. The production and delivery of 8 Tejas Trainer would start after design clearance by ADA.[^cite_note-Orders-179]

The second Tejas Mark 1 squadron, [Squadron 18](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/No._18_Squadron_IAF), was formed at [Sulur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sulur_Air_Force_Station) on 27 May 2020.[^cite_note-tejas2sq-224]

The first front fuselage of Tejas Mk1 FOC aircraft for the second order was delivered by Dynamatic Technologies on 2 November 2020.[^cite_note-225]

By August 2023, the delivery of all 32 single-seat Tejas Mk 1 aircraft (IOC and FOC) were completed.[^cite_note-BS-230823-3]



Tejas MK1 at [Aero India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aero_India) 2025

The first series production trainer aircraft (LT5201) completed its maiden flight on 5 April 2023.[^cite_note-226] IAF has received first LCA Tejas twin seater aircraft from HAL on 4 October 2023.[^cite_note-\:45-227][^cite_note-228] Next trainer variant delivery expected by 31 March.[^cite_note-\:42-229][^cite_note-\:43-230]

As of 18 September 2024, 4 trainer aircraft have been delivered to IAF while the rest of 4 are to be delivered by March 2025.[^cite_note-\:54-180]

As of 10 January 2025, 38 of the 40 aircraft have been delivered and the last two trainers would be delivered soon.[^cite_note-\:56-182] On 11 January 2025, the last of the 40 Tejas - LT-5208 - took its first flight.[^cite_note-X-27032422-183][^cite_note-X-2703242-184]

As of 5 February 2025, the final two trainer aircraft remains undelivered since they are yet to meet the Air Service Qualitative Requirements (ASQRs) of the IAF.[^cite_note-\:40-185] On 18 September 2026, the seventh and eighth trainers were delivered to the Indian Air Force which completed the first two Tejas orders placed in 2006 and 2010, respectively.[^cite_note-\:84-4][^cite_note-\:85-188]

#### First Mark 1A contract delays

As of February 2021, three units of Tejas Mk 1A was to be delivered in the third year from contract signing (2024) followed by a production rate of 16 aircraft per year for five years, completing deliveries within eight years of contract signing. The first aircraft will have an indigenous content of 50% which will be elevated to 60% by the end of contract.[^cite_note-\:61-192]

As of October 2022, the delivery date of the first aircraft was reportedly scheduled on 3 February 2024 with three aircraft to be delivered by 31 March.[^cite_note-231] In December 2023, it was reported that the IAF has plans to raise the first squadron of Tejas Mark 1A at [Nal Air Force Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bikaner_Airport).[^cite_note-232] Later in June 2024, it was confirmed that [No. 3 Squadron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/No._3_Squadron_IAF) and [No. 23 Squadron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/No._23_Squadron_IAF) would be the first units to operate the Mk 1A variant replacing their in-service [MiG 21s](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MiG_21).[^cite_note-\:50-233]

The delivery of F-404 engines, which were scheduled to begin from February 2024 have been delayed, due to supply chain issues, as claimed by [GE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Electric).[^cite_note-Hindu-29102024-234] The delivery of the first aircraft itself was shifted to the end of March 2024.[^cite_note-theprint.in-235] By then, the IAF's latest requests for software upgrades and minor design changes caused the delay in delivery timeline, according to sources within the military and defence establishment. Also, a number of certifications were pending. The delivery of essential components that were ordered from Israel, Russia and Ukraine have also been delayed due to the [Russian invasion of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine). All of which is impacting the production and delivery schedule.[^cite_note-theprint.in-235][^cite_note-236]

In May 2024, the [MoD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Defence_\(India\)) asked HAL in May to deliver at least 18 Tejas Mk 1A by March 2025 and the entire 180 jets (including later 97 Tejas Mk 1A order) within 10 years.[^cite_note-237][^cite_note-X-270324-238] Subsequently, the delivery dates were shifted several times from February 2024 to July-end (as of May),[^cite_note-239][^cite_note-240] to 15 August (as of July)[^cite_note-\:51-241] and again to November (as of August).[^cite_note-\:52-242]

In August, the first aircraft was undergoing critical flight tests with weapons trials to be expected soon while the second was in high speed taxi trials phase. The next 4 units were in advanced stages of production. HAL then planned to manufacture at least 14 airframes in "ready-to-go state" by the [fiscal year](5752099.md) in order to ensure swift engine integration and aircraft deliveries within few weeks after arrival of new engines.[^cite_note-\:52-242]

Again, in September 2024, [Denmark](76972.md) prohibited the export of engine charge amplifiers, previously installed on Tejas Mk 1, resulting in further delays of Tejas Mk 1A deliveries. Later, HAL assigned a Bengaluru-based company to indigenously develop the component which would then be tested before integration. This is a crucial component tasked to sense engine temperature and adjust operation accordingly.[^cite_note-243]

The same month, a report suggested that the fighter aircraft's Israeli software integration being finished and is undergoing final trials and deliveries could begin from October 2024. The delivery could begin with Category B engines, referring to the engines previously used or procured from GE. The F404-IN20 engine delivery had been shifted to arrive in November 2024.[^cite_note-\:55-244]

There was an additional problem for the delivery. [General Electric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Electric) had closed down the production line of F404-IN20 at [Lynn, Massachusetts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lynn,_Massachusetts) engines earlier without further prospects of orders from India and the first engine was yet to be delivered even in October 2024. First engine delivery dates were delayed to September 2024 as of then and followed by a production rate of two per month.[^cite_note-\:51-241][^cite_note-245] By November, engine deliveries was delayed by 10 months.[^cite_note-246] Engine deliveries were again shifted to April 2025 though uncertainties remained. The reason for delay was cited as supply chain issues since a South Korean firm with financial issues failed to deliver certain components of the engine. HAL had 5-6 aircraft ready for delivery. The Mk 1A is in the final stages of trials before delivery.[^cite_note-Hindu-29102024-234][^cite_note-247][^cite_note-248] Following the orders, a lengthy process of sorting out supply chain problems was undertaken and the F404 production line at [Boston](24437894.md) restarted by late 2024 to enable deliveries after a stagnation of 5 years.[^cite_note-\:532-249][^cite_note-250] As of 10 January 2025, GE had a backlog of 26 engines.[^cite_note-251][^cite_note-\:56-182]

As of late December, further critical trials of Tejas Mk 1A using Category B F404 engines were to commence in January 2025 as a part of a [contingency plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contingency_plan). The new deadline for delivery of first aircraft was set for 31 March 2025. The older engines would be replaced once the new engines are delivered. The trials will also include [Astra Mk1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Astra_\(missile\)) [beyond visual range air-to-air missiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beyond-visual-range_air-to-air_missile) (BVRAAM), [EL/M-2052](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EL/M-2052) and indigenous [Electronic Warfare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electronic_Warfare) Suite ([radar warning receiver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radar_warning_receiver) and advanced self-protection jammer). The aircraft will be delivered after certain certifications are done.[^cite_note-252][^cite_note-\:532-249]

As of 6 February 2025, HAL had three Tejas Mk 1A aircraft "ready in the flight line" with another two to be prepared by March–April. Deliveries were expected within few days after GE starts engine deliveries in March. Integration of EW Suite and radars was complete while trials of Astra missiles form these jets was expected soon. GE "promised" to deliver 12 engines in 2025-26 starting in March 2025 with 20 engines to be delivered from 2026-27 onwards. Tejas delivery rate will depend on the revised engine delivery schedules. Meanwhile, Nashik line is reportedly operational and the first Tejas Mk 1A is planned to roll out in one or two months.[^cite_note-\:58-253][^cite_note-\:59-254] Secretary of Defence Production Sanjeev Kumar stated during [Aero India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aero_India)'s [2025 edition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Aero_India_Editions#15th_edition_\(2025\)) that Tejas' production line was stabilized, both with HAL and GE engines, and that Tejas' supply will follow the original plan starting in 2025–2026 when 16 to 24 jets would be delivered.[^cite_note-\:59-254][^cite_note-255]

On 9 March 2025, the first rear [fuselage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fuselage) for Tejas Mk 1A was delivered to HAL by Alpha Tocol Engineering Services. Also, 12 rear fuselages manufactured by HAL was also ready on production line.[^cite_note-256][^cite_note-257]

As of 16 March 2025, the first Tejas Mk 1A from the Nashik (Ozar) production line is expected to be rolled out in April.[^cite_note-\:60-216] On 22 March, a report confirmed that, after two years of delay, the General Electric was preparing to deliver the first engine by March-end from the contracted 99 GE F404-IN20 engines as it was undergoing flight tests in their [testbed aircraft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Testbed_aircraft). Further, GE plans to deliver a total of 12 engines in 2025 followed by a steady rate of only 20 engines per year from 2026 onwards.[^cite_note-258]

After a two-year delay from the anticipated timeframes, the first of 99 F404-IN20 engines was rolled out from GE's factory on 25 March 2025, signaling the start of deliveries.[^cite_note-259][^cite_note-260] The engine will be further tested at the HAL plant when it arrives in India in April. HAL produced three aircraft in 2025, will receive twelve engines from GE, and plans to produce eleven more aircraft by the end of 2025.[^cite_note-261]

As of 30 March, Tejas Mk 1A was undergoing extra firing and EW trials. Also, the first Tejas Mk 1A is expected to be delivered from Nashik facility in the first quarter of FY 2025–26 after the HAL gets physical possession of the first engine in India in early April.[^cite_note-262] Tejas Mk 1A failed an Astra Mk1 trial during the trials.[^cite_note-\:67-190]

On 30 May, the first centre fuselage by [Hyderabad](37534.md)-based VEM Technologies for Tejas Mk 1A was delivered to HAL. Another assembly jig to manufacture the component has also been cleared. The centre fuselage of the aircraft is a [semi-monocoque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Semi-monocoque) construction with both metallic and [carbon fiber composite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carbon_fiber_composite) material.[^cite_note-263][^cite_note-264] This was followed by the delivery of second centre fuselage on 4 September 2025. Each unit needs a total of 1,595 parts to be manufactured before they are assembled. HAL officials inspect every component at every stage of their production. The firm is scheduled to deliver a total of five fuselage units by year-end.[^cite_note-\:64-265][^cite_note-\:65-266]

As of June 2025, the first aircraft is now expected to be delivered by the end of the month from the Nashik plant.[^cite_note-267][^cite_note-268] Further, HAL chief revealed that the company has six jets ready on the production line, all of which are to be delivered to IAF by March 2026. Also, 12 engines in addition to the one delivered so far are expected to be delivered by General Electric to HAL within the same timeframe while the latter also expects to deliver 16 jets in fiscal year 2026-27 given that there is a steady engine supply from GE.[^cite_note-269][^cite_note-270]

On 8 July, PK Mishra, principal secretary to the Prime Minister, visited the HAL's LCA Tejas Assembly Hangar and Aerospace Division to review the progress of the programme. Mishra was shown that six Tejas Mk 1A single-seat and two twin-seat trainer variants on the assembly line fitted with "some other test engines" which would be replaced by GE F404-IN20 engines when delivered. Additionally, the two new engines will reportedly arrive in the same month, as per reports.[^cite_note-271][^cite_note-272] Meanwhile, [Defence Secretary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defence_Secretary_\(India\)) [Rajesh Kumar Singh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rajesh_Kumar_Singh_\(civil_servant\)) also confirmed that GE will deliver two engines per month from July 2025 to March 2026. The next engine is to be delivered by the end of the month. While HAL has limited Category B engines, these available engines are being rotated among new aircraft to conduct test flights.[^cite_note-273]

On 13 July, it was reported that the second engine had been dispatched for delivery to HAL. The unit reached India on 14 July.[^cite_note-274][^cite_note-275] Additionally, [Larsen & Toubro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Larsen_&_Toubro) (L\&T) completed the construction of the first pair of private sector-manufactured wings. Reportedly, the official delivery was undertaken with a ceremony on 17 July at Precision Engineering & Systems Complex of [Coimbatore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coimbatore), [Tamil Nadu](29918.md). Meanwhile, the three sections of the fuselage have been already delivered by the respective private sector counterparts. With almost the entire aero structure produced by private sector companies, HAL will remain the final integrator and will ensure the assembly, testing, and certification of Tejas at its Final Assembly Lines at Nashik and Bangalore.[^cite_note-276][^cite_note-277] As reported on 28 July, the aircraft were moving towards its "next phase" and is ready to "roll out". The testing of [Astra Mk1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Astra_\(missile\)) [BVRAAM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Braamfontein) is scheduled for early August 2025.[^cite_note-278]

On 11 August, the Directorate General of Aeronautical Quality Assurance (DGAQA) approved the flight clearance for the Tejas Mk 1A aircraft produced by HAL's Nashik division. The Additional Director General, AQA, also formally handed over the Flight Clearance Document to the executive director of the Nashik division. The Office of the Additional Director General (OADG), a field establishment of DGAQA, in Nashik was also responsible for the [Transfer of Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transfer_of_Technology) of LCA Mk 1A to the Nashik division.[^cite_note-\:62-221][^cite_note-279] As of 19 August, the first two of the jets were "fully ready" and their final weapons trial was due by September.[^cite_note-\:63-201]

The third engine is scheduled to be delivered in August as per GE's revised delivery timeline of one engine per month, followed by two engines monthly from October onwards.[^cite_note-\:4-200] However, GE missed its scheduled delivery of two units in the month and has committed to deliver three to five engineers by October.[^cite_note-\:66-280]

As of 30 August, 10 airframes of Tejas Mk 1A are ready with 2 of them equipped with newly delivered engines, including one from the Nashik plant.[^cite_note-\:66-280] As of now, both of them are expected to be delivered by September-end and it is only after the delivery of two aircraft that the contract of additional 97 Tejas Mk 1A would be signed.[^cite_note-281] In early September, the dates were again pushed to October, still subject to the jets' weapons trials, including Astra missile, [ASRAAM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ASRAAM) and [laser-guided bomb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sudarshan_laser-guided_bomb) trials, which is underway[^cite_note-\:66-280] is completed in September itself.[^cite_note-\:64-265][^cite_note-\:65-266]

The third engine to HAL was delivered on 11 September with another engine to be supplied by month end. An overall of 12 engines are to be delivered in this fiscal year. In the current year, the Nashik plant could prepare 3-4 aircraft with the numbers reaching 8 units from the following year. Also, the private sector supply chain will enhance HAL's annual production capacity by 6 aircraft to 30.[^cite_note-282]

As of 16 September, there are overall 24 airframes in the production lines combined of which eight are ready for delivery while two have been integrated with the new engines. Seven further engines would be delivered to HAL this year followed by 20 units next year. The two aircraft began their weapons trials involving ASRAAM missiles in the country's eastern sector which will be followed by Astra trials. After the Astra firing from Tejas Mk 1A in late March failed, the [DRDL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defence_Research_and_Development_Laboratory) was compelled to undertake certain software tweaks to address the shortcomings and HAL is now awaiting approval from [CEMILAC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CEMILAC)'s safety review board to continue with its trials.[^cite_note-\:67-190] As of 25 September, it was reported that the deliveries could be delayed until the first quarter of 2026. This is because of the Air Force's mandate of accepting only fully ready fighter aircraft with four distinct validations including firing validation of specialised munitions, integration of Electronic Warfare suite among others. However, these validations is not complete yet.[^cite_note-\:68-107]

As on 30 September, the maiden flight of the first aircraft from the Nashik plant is expected in October.[^cite_note-283] On 1 October, the fourth engine was delivered to HAL.[^cite_note-\:69-206][^cite_note-284][^cite_note-285] By 3 October, the weapons firing trials of the first two aircraft was completed, which included Astra and ASRAAM missiles trials, and were ready to be delivered following necessary certifications.[^cite_note-286][^cite_note-\:71-287]

The third production line of Tejas Mk1A along with the second production line of [HTT-40](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HTT-40) at HAL's Nashik facility was inaugurated by the Defence Minister on 17 October,[^cite_note-\:74-217][^cite_note-\:75-218] as was reported on 8 October.[^cite_note-\:73-219][^cite_note-\:71-287] The preceding production lines of both aircraft is based in Bengaluru. The first Mark 1A (LA-5043)[^cite_note-\:72-288] from the Nashik plant conducted its maiden flight and had already completed its assembly and pre-flight trials.[^cite_note-\:72-288] A flying formation of Tejas, [Su-30MKI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Su-30MKI) and [HTT-40](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HTT-40) was exhibited on the occasion. The Mark 1A aircraft also received a [water cannon salute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_cannon_salute).[^cite_note-\:74-217][^cite_note-\:75-218] Additionally, the first aircraft was expected to be delivered in the last week of the month,[^cite_note-\:73-219][^cite_note-\:71-287] along with the scheduled delivery of two engines within the month.[^cite_note-\:71-287]

The delivery deadline was again delayed to March 2026. Several Air Force officials reportedly expects further delays of over six months as multiple checks and pre-delivery procedures are due.[^cite_note-289] Although HAL was scheduled to deliver 10 Tejas Mk 1A aircraft to the Indian Air Force in FY2025-26, the numbers were revised to five in December 2025.[^cite_note-290]

The second set of wings from Larsen & Toubro was delivered on 31 December 2025. The firm will supply four wing sets per annum which can be enhanced to 12 sets subject to demand.[^cite_note-291][^cite_note-292]

The missile and firing trials are completed according to HAL chief as of 28 January 2026.[^cite_note-293] The trials included two missile launches and release of a [laser-guided bomb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laser-guided_bomb). The Air Staff Qualitative Requirements (ASQR) certification and operational clearances, which are mandatory before formal induction, are still due. The SQRs include flight envelope validation, avionics and sensor integration, electronic warfare performance, reliability and maintainability parameters and operational suitability checks. HAL will approach IAF to take the deliveries within the fiscal year. Thereafter, IAF will decide on its acceptance and handover details.[^cite_note-294]

As of 4 February, 15 airframes are in ready configuration state and will be joined by 5 more within this fiscal year. However, the Indian Air Force will reportedly review the Tejas Mk 1A programme in May before deciding on the acceptance of delivery of these planes. A detailed discussion on the same was done in December 2025.[^cite_note-295] On 5 February, HAL further clarified through a [press release](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Press_release) that while five aircraft, "incorporating major contracted capabilities" under the ASQR, are ready for deliveries, nine more have completed their first flights and awaits GE engine deliveries. Only five engines had been received and the design and development issues are being resolved in an "expedited manner".[^cite_note-296][^cite_note-\:8-5] However, [Business Standard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Business_Standard) reported that the firm has sought some relaxations on the ASQR to meet the March 2026 deadline for five jets. Hence, even if the jets are delivered within the deadline, they will lack full operational capabilities. If the requests are partially agreed to, the delays might extend to May, or even June–July if all the ASQR are to be met. The IAF has raised a list of operational and safety issues that needs to be sorted before acceptance of deliveries. One of the operational issue was the simultaneous functioning of the radar with the other systems including the electronic warfare suite.[^cite_note-297]

On the first week of February, reports emerged revealing the post-coupling of 19th LCA Mk1A (LA-5051) have been completed and the airframe has been rolled out.[^cite_note-298]

As reported on 21 February, the IAF has laid down a list of essentials before accepting the aircraft, however, it has agreed to some exemption of the contractual obligations after consulting with the defence ministry. This implied that the jets will incorporate some systems even after delivery. The essentials included the final missile firing trial, certification, full integration of radar and EW systems and the complete weapons package. HAL received the list of essential tasks in a meeting with IAF which it agreed to complete by April and followed by a few weeks-long acceptance trial by the IAF. The delivery dates were shifted to the new [fiscal year](5752099.md).[^cite_note-299]

On 1 April 2026, it was reported that a critical programme review would be undertaken by the IAF and HAL. In the process, the IAF would take an update of the progress and finalise a revised delivery schedule. Officials from HAL and the IAF would meet in [New Delhi](51585.md). The outcome of the review would be decisive. If the programme was "sufficiently on track" the deliveries would be taken within two to three months, otherwise there would be further slippages. The five aircraft deemed to be ready by HAL had only "major" contracted capabilities incorporated and multiple contracted capabilities, including the mandatory ones, are yet to be certified.[^cite_note-300]

Meanwhile, GE is expected to deliver the sixth engine, which is ready, to HAL by month-end and another 20 units between June and December 2026. Hence, there would be six Tejas Mk1A with Cat-A engines by April-end. Twenty Tejas Mk1A airframes has also kept ready. Delivery would begin when [ASRAAM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ASRAAM) trials are completed in the required configuration and some radar software upgrades. HAL is also implementing the contractual penalties on GE, in the form of [liquidated damages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liquidated_damages), for every delay in engine deliveries under a provision stated in the contract. GE has also reportedly assured deliveries of over 20 engines from 2027.[^cite_note-301][^cite_note-\:76-302]

The critical programme review was yet to be held as per an 11 May report. The meeting at New Delhi would see participation of senior IAF and HAL officials to assess progress on mandatory operational benchmarks before service induction and finalise revised programme delivery timelines. The delay was because the meeting was contingent on HAL updating the IAF on progress in resolving pending technical issues which was due.[^cite_note-303] The programme review was pushed to June without a firm date with the deliveries were delayed to August–September. This was announced by HAL on 15 May. Even though IAF was willing to accept further relaxation to capability inclusion, the MoD would need to implement some tweaks in the contract.[^cite_note-\:77-304][^cite_note-\:78-305]

Key challenges include seamless integration of the AESA radar, electronic warfare suite and mission systems through the aircraft’s mission computer network. Additional testing, software corrections, missile firing trials and full weapons package validation are still required before full operational clearance. IAF is also willing to accept aircraft deliveries even if some electronic warfare automation targets remain incomplete, provided full capability is delivered later. Pilots can temporarily perform such functions manually, as was common in earlier-generation aircraft.[^cite_note-\:77-304][^cite_note-\:78-305]

The programme review meeting was conducted on 8 June 2026. All defence projects of HAL were reviewed with special emphasis on LCA Mk1A and the firm was asked to strictly adhere to timelines. The defence ministry is also examining a potential financial penalty for the delays. So far, 18 Mk1A airframes have been prepared and six engines have been delivered.[^cite_note-306] The ministry expects 18 to 24 LCA Mk1A to be ready for delivery by year-end under the production and readiness schedule if the remaining ASQR are met.[^cite_note-307] In 26 June, IAF was expected to make further exemptions on mandatory capabilities to expedite deliveries. Meanwhile, HAL was expected to solve remaining integration problems involving its AESA radar. The MoD is also examining a move to impose a financial penalty on HAL for the delays.[^cite_note-308] GE delivered the seventh engine on 13 July.[^cite_note-309]

In August 2026, three engines were delivered by GE, taking the total deliveries to 10 units.[^cite_note-310] In September 2026, the deliveries are expected to commence in December with 10 aircraft to be delivered in fiscal year 2026-27. By then, construction, maiden flight and tests of 27 airframe has been completed; while issues regarding radar integration are being resolved with final weapons trials and some tests related to confirming standards[^cite_note-\:85-188] are pending.[^cite_note-\:83-204][^cite_note-311] [Air Marshal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air_marshal_\(India\)) [Ashutosh Dixit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashutosh_Dixit), the [Vice Chief of the Air Staff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vice_Chief_of_the_Air_Staff_\(India\)), also confirmed that the jets are undergoing flight & systems trials to validate final two to three technical parameters are left related to a software integration problem which could be solved within one or two months. A report form *[The New Indian Express](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_New_Indian_Express)* stated that the IAF would only accept aircraft when the ASQR is fully met. The issue is to integrate and synchronise the AESA radar with the electronic warfare (EW) suite and other onboard mission softwares via the mission computer network. Some of the final parameters are undergoing refinement and validation.[^cite_note-312][^cite_note-313]

## Operational history

The Tejas Mark 1 made its international debut on 21 January 2016, at the fourth [Bahrain International Airshow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bahrain_International_Airshow).[^cite_note-314]



IAF [Su-30 MKI](3793034.md), French [Rafale](33731380.md), IAF Tejas FOC during [Exercise Garuda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exercise_Garuda) VII

On 26 January 2017, Tejas made its maiden appearance in the 68th [Republic Day parade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delhi_Republic_Day_parade) by joining in the fly-past over the [Kartavya Path](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rajpath) (then Rajpath) in [New Delhi](51585.md).[^cite_note-315]

In April 2018, the IAF's entire fleet of Tejas Mark 1 aircraft participated in the Gagan Shakti 2018 exercise. It was the IAF's largest air exercise, involving 1,100 aircraft and 15,000 military personnel. During the exercise, the Tejas were deployed to forward bases and demonstrated their reliability and precision strike capability.[^cite_note-\:11-31] In 2019, six Tejas fighter jets participated in the Vayu Shakti air exercise, where it has demonstrated its "[swing role](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multirole_combat_aircraft#Swing-role)" capability.[^cite_note-316]

According to the commanding officer of [No. 45 Squadron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/No._45_Squadron_IAF) – [Group Captain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Group_captain_\(India\)) Samrath Dhankhar, DASH IV HMDS enables the Tejas pilot to take full advantage of high off-boresight [close combat missiles](320568.md#Fifth_generation),[^cite_note-317] such as [Python-5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_\(missile\)) and [R-73](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R-73_\(missile\)).[^cite_note-318]

On 18 August 2020, IAF deployed the No. 45 Squadron "Flying Dagger" on the western front along the Pakistani border ([line of control](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Line_of_control)). It was the first operational deployment of Tejas.[^cite_note-319]

On 27 April 2021, Tejas Mark 1 successfully test fired [Python-5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python-5) high off-boresight (HOBS) close combat [air-to-air missile](320568.md) and further validated enhanced capability of [I-Derby ER](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafael_Derby) (extended range) BVR missile. Both missiles scored direct hits on targets during the trial.[^cite_note-\:10-59] IAF participated in Exercise Desert Flag VIII, with five Tejas and two [C-17 Globemaster III](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boeing_C-17_Globemaster_III) aircraft, held in UAE from 27 February to 17 March 2023. This is the first time Tejas participated in an international flying exercise outside India.[^cite_note-320]

Tejas LSP-7 firing [Astra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Astra_\(missile\)) Mk1 missile

On 23 August 2023, Astra Mk-1 Beyond Visual Range air-to-air missile was successfully test fired from HAL Tejas aircraft off the coast of [Goa](40010153.md) from an altitude of 20,000 ft (6.1 km). This is the first time when India test fire an indigenously developed BVRAAM from a homegrown fighter aircraft.[^cite_note-Astra-321][^cite_note-322]

In December 2023, plans were revealed to deploy the first batch of Tejas Mk 1A in the [Nal airbase](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bikaner_Airport) in Bikaner, [Rajasthan](26291.md).[^cite_note-323] [No. 3 Squadron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/No._3_Squadron_IAF) and [No. 23 Squadron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/No._23_Squadron_IAF), the last and current operators of [MiG-21s](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mikoyan-Gurevich_MiG-21), are expected to become the first operators of Tejas Mk 1A. Both the squadrons were deployed in Nal AFS by June 2024. The first delivery of Tejas Mk 1A is expected by November 2024.[^cite_note-\:50-233][^cite_note-\:52-242]



[No. 18 Squadron IAF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/No._18_Squadron_IAF) at [INS Dega](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/INS_Dega) for [Milan Naval Exercise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milan_\(naval_exercise\)) 2024

On 26 January 2024, on the occasion of 75th Republic Day of India, four Tejas took part in the parade fly-past held at [Kartavya Path](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rajpath), [New Delhi](51585.md).[^cite_note-324]



Tejas (LA-5022) of [Squadron No. 18](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/No._18_Squadron_IAF) piloted by [VCOAS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vice_Chief_of_the_Air_Staff_\(India\)) [AP Singh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amar_Preet_Singh) during Exercise Tarang Shakti 2024

HAL Tejas Mk 1 participated in the first phase of [Exercise Tarang Shakti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exercise_Tarang_Shakti) 2024. The jets were escorted by a formation of four HAL Tejas Mk 1 aircraft, one of which was piloted by the then [Vice Chief of the Air Staff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vice_Chief_of_the_Air_Staff_\(India\)), [Air Marshal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air_marshal_\(India\)) [Amar Preet Singh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amar_Preet_Singh). The exercise began with Tejas intercepting German, French and Spanish jets as a part of the training exercise.[^cite_note-\:210-325] HAL Tejas was assigned to the Red Force (hostile) as well as the Blue Force (friendly) and its capabilities were proved in both the cases. The jet also received appreciation from the foreign pilots who piloted them. On 13 August, the [Chief of French Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_of_Staff_of_the_French_Air_and_Space_Force), General [Stéphane Mille](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St%C3%A9phane_Mille?action=edit\&redlink=1) and the Chief of German Air Force [Lieutenant General](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Generalleutnant#Germany) [Ingo Gerhartz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ingo_Gerhartz) piloted HAL Tejas.[^cite_note-326]

The first production aircraft of Tejas Mk 1A was showcased at [Aero India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aero_India) 2025.[^cite_note-\:56-182]

On 12 March 2025, Astra Mk-1 Beyond Visual Range air-to-air missile was successfully test fired from a Tejas Mk1 prototype over the [Integrated Test Range](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Integrated_Test_Range), [Chandipur, Odisha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chandipur,_Odisha). The missile scored a direct hit on a flying target at a range of over 100 km. This test was significant for the subsequent induction of Tejas Mk 1A aircraft. Further trials are also expected for performance evaluation.[^cite_note-Press_Information_Bureau-124]

The aircraft did not participate in [Exercise Vayushakti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exercise_Vayushakti) on 27 February 2026 due to the fleet's grounding in the month following a take-off incident.[^cite_note-TW28022026-327]

Tejas was part of the IAF contingent in Exercise Veer Guardian which was conducted on 9–22 September 2026 at [Jodhpur Air Force Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jodhpur_Air_Force_Station).[^cite_note-328] The [Chief of the Air Staff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_of_the_Air_Staff_\(India\)), [Air Chief Marshal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air_chief_marshal_\(India\)) [Amar Preet Singh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amar_Preet_Singh), and the JASDF chief, [General](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_ranks_and_insignia_of_the_Japan_Self-Defense_Forces#Air_Self-Defense_Force) Takehiro Morita, conducted their respective solo sorties on Tejas simultaneously on 20 September.[^cite_note-329]

## Potential operators

HAL proposed exporting the Tejas, with preliminary talks taking place with several friendly countries.[^cite_note-\:9-330] It was reported in March 2020 that HAL is willing to set up logistic facilities in Indonesia, Malaysia, Sri Lanka and Vietnam as part of exporting the Tejas.[^cite_note-331]

In a July 2026 interview, Hindustan Aeronautics Limited (HAL) Chairman and Managing Director Ravi K. said in an interview that domestic production is being prioritized with local capacity and ecosystem being worked on to fulfill domestic orders before export orders will be done.[^cite_note-332]

### Brazil

In September 2024, the [Brazilian Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_Air_Force) (FAB) has reportedly considered the adoption of the Tejas to replace their [AMX](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AMX_International_AMX) and [Northrop F-5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northrop_F-5) jets in service and complement the [Saab JAS 39 Gripen](87577.md), which is being done with a swap of [Embraer C-390 Millennium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Embraer_C-390_Millennium).[^cite_note-333][^cite_note-334][^cite_note-FAB-335] FAB wanted to acquire [F-16 Fighting Falcon](11642.md) at first from the US, but it was rejected.[^cite_note-336]

According to the FAB, the AMX is due to be retired by 2025 with the F-5s being next by 2030.[^cite_note-FAB-335] In November 2024, Brazil offered to India a swap between the C-390 Millennium and the HAL Tejas, to replace the older F-5s in operation.[^cite_note-337] In December 2024, an Indian delegation visited Brazil to negotiate the deal.[^cite_note-338] It was reported in Brazilian media that talks on getting the Tejas are in advanced stages.[^cite_note-339]

The FAB is also looking into the Rafale to replace their AMX jets.[^cite_note-340]

A decision is expected before the end of 2026.[^cite_note-341]

### DR Congo

In October 2024, a report suggested that the [Air Force of the Democratic Republic of the Congo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air_Force_of_the_Democratic_Republic_of_the_Congo) (FAC) was interested to acquire HAL Tejas.[^cite_note-MAFAC-342] It was reported in August 2024 that a FAC delegation visited India to speak with HAL representatives about the Tejas.[^cite_note-MAFAC-342]

The FAC plans to replace their [Sukhoi Su-25s](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sukhoi_Su-25) with the Tejas if purchased.[^cite_note-343]

### Failed bids

#### Argentina

The [Argentine Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Argentine_Air_Force) had indicated its interest in possibly purchasing the Tejas, as part of its modernization initiative.[^cite_note-auto-147] [HAL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindustan_Aeronautics_Limited) had offered the Tejas to Argentina, amidst other offers of the [JF-17](1928578.md) from [China](5405.md) and the [MiG-35](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mikoyan_MiG-35) from [Russia](25391.md).[^cite_note-344][^cite_note-345] Multiple sources had also indicated that the Tejas may likely be a good option for Argentina.[^cite_note-\:3-21]

However, any potential sale of the Tejas to Argentina may likely be threatened by British [imposed arms sanctions](5974443.md), since the Tejas utilizes the British [Martin-Baker MK16 IN16 GS Tejas ejection seat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin-Baker), along with other British-origin components — including an [aerial-refueling probe](237949.md) and a quartz radome, both supplied by [Cobham Limited](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cobham_\(company\)).[^cite_note-\:79-346][^cite_note-auto-147] The UK has barred any sale of military equipment consisting of British-manufactured parts to Argentina ever since the 1982 [Falklands War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Falklands_War).[^cite_note-347] Argentina's previous efforts to procure modern fighter aircraft, including the [Mirage F1M](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dassault_Mirage_F1), the [IAI Kfir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IAI_Kfir), the [JAS 39 Gripen](87577.md) and the [KAI FA-50](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KAI_T-50_Golden_Eagle) were scuttled due to British diplomatic pressure, since the aforementioned aircraft were found to contain UK-origin parts.[^cite_note-348][^cite_note-en.mercopress.com-349]

Given the nature of the Argentine-specific sanctions, the Tejas would essentially be subject to UK-scrutiny. Nevertheless, HAL reportedly stated multiple times that they are willing to accommodate Argentinian requests to retrofit the Tejas,[^cite_note-350][^cite_note-351] and had offered a customised-variant of the Tejas which replaces around 50-specific components and the substitutes all major British-origin components with diplomatically suitable non-British alternatives,[^cite_note-352] including possibly substituting the ejection seat with one indigenously developed and manufactured in India.[^cite_note-353] However, any customisation to the HAL Tejas would encompass considerable changes to the aircraft's design and additional flight certification.[^cite_note-354] Subsequently, there were indications that the Argentines were willing to look at the JF-17 because of concerns that the time and money required to modify the Tejas to remove non-British parts was beyond what Buenos Aires was willing to spend.[^cite_note-355][^cite_note-356]

In December 2022, the Argentine government decided to abandon the decision to acquire new fighter aircraft.[^cite_note-neither-jf-17-nor-tejas-357] With President [Alberto Fernandez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alberto_Fernandez) stating in an interview that "Argentina has to allocate its resources to more important things than the purchase of military aircraft. We are in a very unequal continent, but there are no war problems and unity among countries is sought."[^cite_note-neither-jf-17-nor-tejas-357][^cite_note-358] In July 2023,[^cite_note-359] Argentine newspaper *La Nacion* reported that Tejas had been eliminated, with the remaining options being second-hand Danish F-16 fighters or Chinese-Pakistani JF-17s, both which the bidding selection board deemed more advantageous.[^cite_note-360] In March 2024, Denmark agreed to the purchase of 24 Danish F-16s by Argentina.[^cite_note-361]

#### Australia

In July 2020, the [Royal Australian Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Australian_Air_Force) (RAAF) had issued a tender, seeking a new jet trainer to replace its fleet of [BAE Hawk 127](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BAE_Systems_Hawk) trainer aircraft.[^cite_note-362] According to HAL's "58th Annual Report" covering 2020–2021, HAL had offered the HAL Tejas in its "Lead in Fighter Trainer" (LIFT) configuration to Australia's [Department of Defence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_of_Defence_\(Australia\)) (DoD) in July 2020.[^cite_note-\:39-363] Other aircraft also reported to be participating in the tender were the [Boeing-Saab T-7 Red Hawk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boeing-Saab_T-7_Red_Hawk), the [Aermacchi M-346 Master](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alenia_Aermacchi_M-346_Master) and the [KAI T-50](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KAI_T-50_Golden_Eagle).[^cite_note-364]

However, the Australian Government rejected a government to government deal proposed by the Indian government for 35 Tejas LIFT aircraft for US\$1 billion.[^cite_note-365] During bilateral defence talks held in September 2021, *[The Indian Express](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Indian_Express)* reported Australia's lack of interest in the offer.[^cite_note-366] In February 2022, the Australian government decided to take up a proposal by BAE to upgrade and refurbish its Hawks with new engines and avionics, extending their lifespan until 2032.[^cite_note-367]

#### Botswana

The [Botswana Defence Force Air Wing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Botswana_Defence_Force_Air_Wing) had approached HAL to procure the Tejas for potentially replacing its [Canadair CF-5s](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadair_CF-5) due to the aircraft's age and problems of acquiring needed parts for maintenance.[^cite_note-AIB-368] The BDFAW had previously considered acquiring Gripens,[^cite_note-369] the F-16,[^cite_note-MAJ-370] KAI T-50,[^cite_note-371] and the Leonardo [M-346](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alenia_Aermacchi_M-346_Master).[^cite_note-AIB-368]

As of July 2024, there were reports that talks on acquiring the Tejas were suspended.[^cite_note-MAT-372] In January 2025, Botswana officially ended negotiations to purchase the Tejas.[^cite_note-thegazette.news-373]

#### Egypt

During the Dubai Airshow 2021, Egyptian officials expressed their interest in procuring 70 LCA Tejas[^cite_note-374] to replace 100 [Hongdu JL-8s](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hongdu_JL-8).[^cite_note-375] Following the Dubai Airshow 2021, HAL and Indian Air Force officials visited Cairo and discussions were going on. As of June 2022, India has offered to set up local production facilities for the LCA Tejas and also for the Light Utility and Light Combat Helicopters in Egypt.[^cite_note-376]

During Aero India 2023, it was reported that Egypt was seeking around 20 multi-role aircraft.[^cite_note-TD-377] HAL previously reported that 35 LCA Mk-1As were being offered in a contract.[^cite_note-TD-377][^cite_note-Bloomberg-378] In August 2024, it was reported that an agreement to purchase the Chinese [Chengdu J-10C](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chengdu_J-10), had been signed.[^cite_note-379]

#### Malaysia

[Malaysia](3607937.md) had frequently indicated that it may be interested in purchasing the HAL Tejas for the [Royal Malaysian Air Force](1137609.md) (RMAF), as part of its attempts to supplement its [MiG-29 fleet](185653.md); reports of Malaysian interest in procuring the aircraft date back to as early as 2019.[^cite_note-380]

In March 2019, the HAL Tejas made its international debut at the [Langkawi International Maritime and Aerospace Exhibition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Langkawi_International_Maritime_and_Aerospace_Exhibition) (LIMA); its presence reportedly generated a great deal of interest, especially by the RMAF.[^cite_note-381] However, in mid-2019, HAL's prospects of exporting the HAL Tejas to Malaysia were severely blemished, owing to [diplomatic tussles between India and Malaysia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India%E2%80%93Malaysia_relations#Modern_history).[^cite_note-382] After the diplomatic skirmishes had ceased in 2020,[^cite_note-383][^cite_note-384] an RMAF delegation reportedly visited HAL's manufacturing-facility at [Bangalore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bangalore) in 2021, to assess the suitability of the HAL Tejas. Possibly in anticipation of a potential order — reaffirming Malaysia's interest in the aircraft.[^cite_note-385][^cite_note-386]

In 2021, the RMAF formally released a tender and [request for proposals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Request_for_proposal) for the supply of 18 light combat-aircraft - labelled as the "Fighter Lead In Trainer-Light Combat Aircraft" (FLIT/LCA), in an effort to supplant its ageing [BAE Hawk 108/208](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BAE_Systems_Hawk) light-combat aircraft and its [MB-339CM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aermacchi_MB-339) trainers.[^cite_note-387][^cite_note-388][^cite_note-theedgemarkets.com-389] Six aircraft were running in the bid for the FLIT/LCA tender — the HAL Tejas MK1A, [KAI FA-50](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KAI_T-50_Golden_Eagle), [HAIC L-15](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hongdu_JL-10), [Aermacchi M-346](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alenia_Aermacchi_M-346_Master), [TAI Hürjet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TAI_H%C3%BCrjet) and [Mikoyan MiG-35](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mikoyan_MiG-35).[^cite_note-theedgemarkets.com-389] Coincidentally, the [JF-17](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PAC/CAC_JF-17_Thunder) — which was reported to be a leading choice for the RMAF, did not participate in the FLIT/LCA tender.[^cite_note-390][^cite_note-391]

In February 2023, the Malaysian government announced that the South Korean bid had won the tender with the Tejas failing to win the contract.[^cite_note-392] Malaysia subsequently ordered 18 KAI FA-50 Block 20 aircraft worth US\$920 million.[^cite_note-393] The FA-50 was reportedly chosen because of it being a tried-and-tested platform in service with numerous countries while the Tejas is largely unproven and untested, with the Indian Air Force being the sole operator. Logistics issues and the FA-50 better meeting the RMAF's FLIT/LCA requirements were also other factors.[^cite_note-394]

#### Nigeria

On 7 December 2023, HAL reported that Nigeria has expressed interest in acquiring the Tejas.[^cite_note-Bloomberg-378] It was reportedly in competition with the JF-17.[^cite_note-395] However, in July 2024, there were reports that talks on acquiring the Tejas were suspended.[^cite_note-MAT-372] In January 2025, Nigeria withdrew from negotiations to purchase the Tejas.[^cite_note-thegazette.news-373] Nigeria instead bought JF 17 from Pakistan which were delivered in 2021.[^cite_note-396]

#### Philippines

In May 2022, India and the Philippines signed an MOU with the [Philippine Aerospace Development Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippine_Aerospace_Development_Corporation),[^cite_note-397] which mentioned the consideration of purchasing the Tejas and other Indian-made aircraft.[^cite_note-398] On 7 July 2022, the Tejas was dropped from further consideration from the multi-role fighter jet (MRF) project. The [F-16V](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Dynamics_F-16_Fighting_Falcon_variants) Block 70/72 and the JAS-39 Gripen C/D+ are currently the main contenders for the Philippine Air Force's MRF project.[^cite_note-399]

In February 2024, HAL reported that it has offered the Tejas Mark 1A to the PADC as a naval strike platform, equipped with the [BrahMos NG](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BrahMos-NG) missile. HAL also agreed to help set up a local partnership for the assembly of the MK1A.[^cite_note-400]

#### Sri Lanka

In 2018, it had been reported that Sri Lanka had shown interest in purchasing the Tejas to replace its aging fleets of [IAI Kfir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IAI_Kfir) and [Chengdu J-7](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chengdu_J-7) aircraft.[^cite_note-401] The intention was for the acquisition of 8 to 12 aircraft to be pursued through a government-government basic agreement.[^cite_note-402]

In 2021, the Sri Lankan government decided to overhaul their Kfirs instead rather than buying new aircraft, which would cost around \$40 million per unit compared to \$49 million in total for overhauling all five Kfirs.[^cite_note-403]

#### United Arab Emirates

The Tejas had attracted interest from the [United Arab Emirates](69328.md) (UAE), with some discussions held during a visit by UAE Minister of State and Defence, Mohammed Ahmed Al Bowardi Al Falacy, during a state visit in October 2018, as part of growing defence relations between India and UAE.[^cite_note-404] By May 2022, Tejas had failed to gain any further interest[^cite_note-405] with the UAE placing an order for 80 Rafale F4s from France.

In February 2023, the UAE signed a deal for purchase of 12 [Hongdu L-15](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hongdu_L-15) trainer and light combat aircraft from China,[^cite_note-406] with options for another 36.[^cite_note-407] UAE officials stated the L-15 order aligned with the UAE's framework for diversifying the military's sources of equipment.[^cite_note-408]

#### United States

In December 2020, in response to a [Request for Information](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Request_for_Information) (RFI) from the [United States Naval Air Systems Command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naval_Air_Systems_Command) (NAVAIR), HAL offered the "Lead In Fighter Trainer" variant of the HAL Tejas — classified as the "HAL Tejas LIFT", to the [United States Navy](20518076.md) (USN), as part the latter's initiative to replace its fleet of [T-45 Goshawk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McDonnell_Douglas_T-45_Goshawk) trainer aircraft, dubbed the "Undergraduate Jet Training System".[^cite_note-409][^cite_note-\:38-410] Other aircraft participating in the UJTS, are the [T-7 Red Hawk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boeing-Saab_T-7_Red_Hawk) — offered by [Boeing](18933266.md) in partnership with [Saab](26968.md), and the [KAI T-50A Golden Eagle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KAI_T-50_Golden_Eagle) — offered by [Korea Aerospace Industries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Korea_Aerospace_Industries) (KAI), in partnership with [Lockheed Martin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lockheed_Martin).[^cite_note-411]

Despite being the only aircraft among the three to be reportedly capable of undertaking operations from an aircraft carrier, the prospects of the USN selecting the HAL Tejas LIFT are reportedly slim, according to multiple sources.[^cite_note-\:38-410][^cite_note-412][^cite_note-413] Several reasons suggested as the cause of the LIFT's bleak prospects in the UJTS are that HAL has refrained from partnering with a US-based company to offer the LIFT (unlike the consortiums of [Boeing](18933266.md)-[Saab](26968.md) and [KAI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Korea_Aerospace_Industries)-[Lockheed Martin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lockheed_Martin), offering the [T-7](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boeing-Saab_T-7_Red_Hawk) and the [T-50A](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KAI_T-50_Golden_Eagle), respectively), a lack of export orders for the type, and its delta-wing design — which makes it less-suitable for low-speed landing.[^cite_note-414]

### Prototypes

Aircraft already built and projected models to be built. Model designations, tail numbers and dates of first flight are shown.

Technology Demonstrators (TD)

- **TD-1** (KH2001) – 4 January 2001.

  [^cite_note-\:7-56]

  [^cite_note-415]

- **TD-2** (KH2002) – 6 June 2002.

  [^cite_note-\:7-56]

Prototype Vehicles (PV)

- **PV-1** (KH2003) – First flight on 25 November 2003.

  [^cite_note-416]

- **PV-2** (KH2004) – First flight on 1 December 2005.

- **PV-3** (KH2005) – First flight on 1 December 2006.

- **PV-5** (KH-T2009) – First flight on 26 November 2009 – Fighter/Trainer variant.

  [^cite_note-\:7-56]

- **PV-6** (KH-T2010) – First flight on 8 November 2014 – Fighter/Trainer variant.

  [^cite_note-SP_Guide_publications-7]

Naval Prototypes (NP)

- **NP-1** (KHN-T3001) – Two-seat naval variant for carrier operations. Rolled out in July 2010.

  [^cite_note-\:28-94]

  &#x20;NP-1 made its first flight on 27 April 2012.

  [^cite_note-417]

- **NP-2** (NAVY3002) – Single-seat naval variant. First flight on 7 February 2015 with ski-jump take-off and arrested landing required in [STOBAR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/STOBAR) carrier.

  [^cite_note-auto2-418]

- **NP-5** (NAVY3005) – Two-seat naval variant. First flight on 29 August 2023. Built to production standard incorporating improvements learned from NP-1 and NP-2.

  [^cite_note-IE-200823-419]

  &#x20;It will help in the development of [HAL TEDBF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HAL_TEDBF).

  [^cite_note-IE-8]

Limited Series Production (LSP) aircraft



Tejas LSP-7 (KH2017) firing [Python-5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python-5).



LCA LSP-4 on its maiden test flight

- **LSP-1** (KH2011) – 25 April 2007. This LCA is powered by F404-F2J3 Engine.

  [^cite_note-abh1-420]

  [^cite_note-\:7-56]

- **LSP-2** (KH2012) – 16 June 2008. This is the first LCA fitted with F404-IN20 engine.

  [^cite_note-abh1-420]

- **LSP-3** (KH2013) – 23 April 2010. The first aircraft to have the Hybrid MMR radar

  [^cite_note-aviationweek.com-50]

  &#x20;and will be close to the IOC standard.

- **LSP-4** (KH2014) – June 2010. The first aircraft that was flown in the (Mark 1) configuration that will be delivered to the [Indian Air Force](403355.md).

  [^cite_note-Missile-58]

  &#x20;The aircraft flew with the Hybrid MMR, a [Countermeasure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Countermeasure) Dispensing System, and an [identify friend or foe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Identification_friend_or_foe) electronic system.

  [^cite_note-421]

- **LSP-5** (KH2015) – 19 November 2010. IOC standard, with all sensors including night lighting in the cockpit, and an auto-pilot.

  [^cite_note-422]

- **LSP-7** (KH2017) – First flight on 9 March 2012.

  [^cite_note-423]

- **LSP-8** (KH2018) – First flight trial completed in March 2013. LSP 8 is the final version upon which production is based.

  [^cite_note-newindianexpress.com-9]

### Production variants



HAL Tejas MK1 FOC at the 2022 [Singapore Airshow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Singapore_Airshow)

- **Tejas Mark 1 IOC** − Single-seat operational variant for the Indian Air Force. 16 aircraft have been delivered in IOC standard constituting [No. 45 Squadron IAF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/No._45_Squadron_IAF).

- **Tejas Mark 1 FOC** −Adding a Cobham refuelling pod and Gryazev-Shipunov GSh-23 auto-cannon, to the IOC specification. Delivery of the Tejas Mark 1 in FOC standard has begun and [18 Squadron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/No._18_Squadron_IAF) (*Flying Bullets*) was equipped with the first aircraft in May 2020.

  [^cite_note-HinduTejasSqn2-73]

  &#x20;FOC standard Tejas Mark 1 are [BVRAAM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BVRAAM) capable, with general flight envelope expansion, increased angle of attack, higher g-limit of +9 g,

  [^cite_note-\:6-424]

  &#x20;updated avionics and flight control software suite, as well as capable of hot refueling and [aerial refueling](237949.md).

  [^cite_note-HinduTejasSqn2-73]

  &#x20;Delivery of all 32 single seat Tejas Mk 1 aircraft is complete.

  [^cite_note-BS-230823-3]

- **Tejas Trainer** − Two-seat operational conversion trainer for the Indian Air Force; also act as LiFT (Lead-in Fighter Trainer) and ground-attack aircraft.

- **Tejas Mark 1A** − An enhanced Tejas Mark 1 equipped with [EL/M-2052](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EL/M-2052) and [Uttam AESA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uttam_AESA) radar, [self-protection jammer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radar_jamming_and_deception), radar warning receiver, as well as being able to mount an external [ECM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electronic_countermeasure) pod.

  [^cite_note-425]

  [^cite_note-auto3-1]

  &#x20;The first Limited Series Production (LSP) aircraft of the Mark.1A variant rolled out in April 2022, and meant to be used as a Flying Testbed (FTB). A second LSP rolled out in late 2022, and shall be used to validate design changes meant to make the Mark.1A variant lighter than the Mark.1, and to optimize weight distribution.

  [^cite_note-426]

  [^cite_note-\:42-229]

  [^cite_note-\:43-230]

### Future developments

- **SPORT** - Supersonic Omni-Role Trainer (SPORT) aircraft is a two-seater [Lead-in Fighter Training](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lead-in_fighter_training) (LiFT) aircraft being developed from the LCA Trainer Mark 1.

  [^cite_note-\:38-410]

  &#x20;It is being offered to the IAF in lieu of the [HAL HLFT-42](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HAL_HLFT-42) concept, and will be powered by the dry-thrust variant of the [GTRE GTX-35VS Kaveri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GTRE_GTX-35VS_Kaveri) being developed for the [DRDO Ghatak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DRDO_Ghatak). It might also be offered for export purposes as a [light fighter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Light_fighter).

- **[Tejas Mark 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HAL_Tejas_Mk2)** - or Medium Weight Fighter, is an enhanced Tejas Mark 1 design which is expected to have a more powerful engine and an increased payload carrying capacity.

  [^cite_note-\:5-427]

  &#x20;The Tejas Mark 2 will feature an AESA radar, an on-board oxygen generation system and a built-in electronic warfare suite among other improvements to avionics.

  [^cite_note-The_Economic_Times-428]

  &#x20;In January 2019, Air Chief Marshal [Birender Singh Dhanoa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Birender_Singh_Dhanoa) said that the IAF has committed to procure twelve squadrons of Tejas Mark 2 aircraft.

  [^cite_note-429]

- **CATS MAX** - The main component of [HAL Combat Air Teaming System](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HAL_Combat_Air_Teaming_System) (CATS), CATS MAX will be a twin seater Tejas Mark 1A modified with CATS interface to act as the mothership of CATS components. The CATS MAX is to be crewed by a pilot and a weapon system officer (WSO), with the later controlling the CATS.

  [^cite_note-430]

  [^cite_note-Jane's-431]

- **Tejas Trainer** - NP-5 based operational conversion trainer for the Indian Navy, with IFR-probe. Indian Navy was reported to be considering HAL's proposal to reestablish a "Carrier Training Squadron" with 18 Naval Tejas, that would be posted on both of India's carriers and additionally serve as point defence interceptors.

### Cancelled variants

- **Tejas Mark 1 Navy** - Naval Variant based on HAL Tejas Mark 1 powered by F404 engine. Cancelled in favour of the new twin-engine naval fighter [HAL TEDBF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HAL_TEDBF).

  [^cite_note-indianexpress.com-432]

- **Tejas Mark 2 Navy** - Proposed naval variant based on the [Tejas Mk 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HAL_Tejas_Mk2). Cancelled in favour of the [HAL TEDBF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HAL_TEDBF).

- **Tejas Trainer IN** - Two-seat operational conversion trainer for the Indian Navy. Cancelled in favour of the HAL TEDBF.

## Accidents and incidents

Since its first flight in 2001, two Tejas aircraft have been lost in accidents, including one fatal case:

- **12 March 2024**: A Tejas Mk 1 aircraft from the [No. 18 Squadron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/No._18_Squadron_IAF) of the Indian Air Force crashed near [Jaisalmer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jaisalmer) in Rajasthan, India after taking part in a tri-service exercise, *Bharat Shakti*. The pilot ejected safely and no one was harmed.

  [^cite_note-438]

  [^cite_note-439]

  &#x20;The crash was the first for the Tejas since the project's inception.

  [^cite_note-Fact-27]

  [^cite_note-440]

  &#x20;In July 2024, officials involved in the investigation stated that an oil pump failure may have led to an engine seizure, that resulted in the crash.

  [^cite_note-441]

* **21 November 2025**: A Tejas Mk 1 aircraft from the No. 18 Squadron of the Indian Air Force, in service with the IAF since 2020,

  [^cite_note-442]

  &#x20;crashed during the [Dubai Airshow](3195918.md) held at the [Al Maktoum International Airport](3260694.md) in [Dubai](211583.md), United Arab Emirates while performing a [barrel roll](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barrel_roll) maneuver, and the pilot was killed in the incident.

  [^cite_note-443]

  [^cite_note-444]

  &#x20;The Indian Air Force announced the constitution of a [court of inquiry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Court_of_inquisition) to investigate the cause of the accident.

  [^cite_note-445]

- **7 February 2026**: A Tejas Mk 1 of the Indian Air Force was involved in an incident at the [Naliya Air Force Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naliya_Air_Force_Station) while taking off for a training sortie. The pilot sustained minor injuries and there was no ejection. Preliminary reports indicate a technical glitch and onboard systems failure leading to a "suspected brake failure". The aircraft then [overshot the runway](8731144.md) and slid into a mud patch softened by prior rainfall. After this incident, the entire fleet of Tejas has been grounded for inspection.

  [^cite_note-446]

  [^cite_note-447]

  [^cite_note-448]

  &#x20;HAL later clarified the incident as a minor technical glitch which is being resolved with the IAF.

  [^cite_note-449]

  [^cite_note-450]

  [^cite_note-451]

  &#x20;It was reported following an investigation that the crash was caused by a software glitch of the aircraft's onboard systems. A software was upgraded jointly with the IAF and HAL and was being "tried out" as of 12 March. The entire fleet will eventually receive the software upgrade. The remaining 36 jets, including 6 trainers, underwent mechanical and metallurgical "exhaustive tests" which included checking the metallurgy of the under carriage that holds the wheels, the electro-magnetic system used for applying brakes and the software.

  [^cite_note-452]

  [^cite_note-453]

  &#x20;As per reports on 2 April, the 36-aircraft fleet is expected to receive flying clearance by 8 April. HAL received the clearance from the local modification committee (LMC). Before the final clearance to return to active service, the aircraft will undergo one-time checks..

  [^cite_note-454]

  [^cite_note-455]

  [^cite_note-\:76-302]
